People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.

Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.

At home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.

It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.

And the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.

Sometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.

Like I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.

I keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.

Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.

I’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.

I mean, when I notice it happening, I get this tight feeling because I can see people getting lost, like their eyes change, and then I’m trying to pull the sentence back but it’s already gone somewhere else. I’ll start with the overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer or paint colors or patient charts, and I know that’s not what I meant to say. It makes me really anxious because I can’t keep it in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I can’t keep it in a straight line anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people would probably say it sounds unlikely. But I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like moved me around to see how I’d react, and they watch me on the unit cameras to check if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, because nights are always short and then someone calls out, and then the schedule gets all messy, and I know that happens, but it doesn’t feel random. It feels like they’re looking at me specifically. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.

The supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.

Like I’ll start trying to explain something from my overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and people are just staring at me like they don’t know where I went. And I can see them getting lost, which makes me feel worse, because I’m trying to say one thing but it comes out all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Is this happening to you all the time, or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: It’s… not every single second, I guess, but it’s happening a lot. Like enough that people notice. I’ll be trying to answer one thing, like something from the overnight shift, and then I’m suddenly on soccer, or the wall color, or charts, and I can see their faces change because they don’t know how I got there. Then I try to fix it and it kind of makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you notice your thoughts or words becoming disorganized like that?\n\nPatient: A lot. I mean, when I notice it happening, I get this tight feeling because I can see people getting lost, like their eyes change, and then I’m trying to pull the sentence back but it’s already gone somewhere else. I’ll start with the overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer or paint colors or patient charts, and I know that’s not what I meant to say. It makes me really anxious because I can’t keep it in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, sometimes I think I hear something and then it’s just the hallway, or the carts, or somebody talking near the nurses’ station, and then I start thinking about whether I checked the room or not. But seeing things, like, I don’t think so. I’m not sure what counts, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts organized when you're not talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not talking, it’s like that in my head too. I’ll try to think about one thing, like what happened on the overnight shift, and then it jumps—soccer, the paint on the wall, charts I was supposed to look at—and I’m trying to pull it back but it doesn’t stay. That’s kind of what brings me in, I guess. I can’t keep it in a straight line anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people would probably say it sounds unlikely. But I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like moved me around to see how I’d react, and they watch me on the unit cameras to check if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, because nights are always short and then someone calls out, and then the schedule gets all messy, and I know that happens, but it doesn’t feel random. It feels like they’re looking at me specifically. Like the camera is there anyway, but it’s… it’s for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisors are watching you through the cameras for any reason other than to ensure patient safety or manage the unit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know the cameras are supposed to be for safety, that’s what everyone says. But it doesn’t feel like just that. It feels like they’re watching me specifically, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I’ll mess up when they move my shifts around.\n\nAnd people keep saying it’s staffing, like someone called out or nights are short, and I get that, I’ve worked those nights, you know, the whole floor gets weird when you’re down one person… but I’m sure they changed mine on purpose. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. I know people say, “Oh, we’re short, somebody called out,” and that happens, I get it, I’ve been there when the whole unit is running around and everyone’s annoyed because nobody brought enough coffee or whatever—but it feels like mine gets changed for a reason.\n\nLike they’re watching how I handle it. The cameras too… they’re supposed to be for the unit, for safety, but I feel like they’re on me. Like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.

Like the camera is there anyway, but it’s… it’s for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisors are watching you through the cameras for any reason other than to ensure patient safety or manage the unit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know the cameras are supposed to be for safety, that’s what everyone says. It feels like they’re watching me specifically, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I’ll mess up when they move my shifts around.\n\nAnd people keep saying it’s staffing, like someone called out or nights are short, and I get that, I’ve worked those nights, you know, the whole floor gets weird when you’re down one person… but I’m sure they changed mine on purpose. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. I know people say, “Oh, we’re short, somebody called out,” and that happens, I get it, I’ve been there when the whole unit is running around and everyone’s annoyed because nobody brought enough coffee or whatever—but it feels like mine gets changed for a reason.\n\nLike they’re watching how I handle it. The cameras too… they’re supposed to be for the unit, for safety, but I feel like they’re on me. Like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.\n\nIt’s more at the unit. Like, I’m sure the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the cameras. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but that feels too easy, like that’s just what they say so I don’t notice. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.\n\nIt’s more at the unit. Like, I’m sure the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the cameras. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but that feels too easy, like that’s just what they say so I don’t notice. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been hearing voices when there's no one around?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… not like someone standing there talking to me, I don’t think. Sometimes I hear something and I turn my head, but then it’s maybe a cart in the hallway or the vents, because those vents make weird sounds, especially at night.

At home it’s quieter, so I notice every little noise. The fridge clicks and then I’m thinking about the unit again, and whether I forgot something, and then I lose track of what I was doing. But voices… I’m not sure I’d call it voices. I haven’t really noticed that like a clear person talking when nobody’s there.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F22, F21\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. Like I’ll start trying to explain something from my overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and people are just staring at me like they don’t know where I went. And I can see them getting lost, which makes me feel worse, because I’m trying to say one thing but it comes out all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Is this happening to you all the time, or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: It’s… not every single second, I guess, but it’s happening a lot. Like enough that people notice. I’ll be trying to answer one thing, like something from the overnight shift, and then I’m suddenly on soccer, or the wall color, or charts, and I can see their faces change because they don’t know how I got there. Then I try to fix it and it kind of makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you notice your thoughts or words becoming disorganized like that?\n\nPatient: A lot. I mean, when I notice it happening, I get this tight feeling because I can see people getting lost, like their eyes change, and then I’m trying to pull the sentence back but it’s already gone somewhere else. I’ll start with the overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer or paint colors or patient charts, and I know that’s not what I meant to say. It makes me really anxious because I can’t keep it in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, sometimes I think I hear something and then it’s just the hallway, or the carts, or somebody talking near the nurses’ station, and then I start thinking about whether I checked the room or not. But seeing things, like, I don’t think so. I’m not sure what counts, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts organized when you're not talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not talking, it’s like that in my head too. I’ll try to think about one thing, like what happened on the overnight shift, and then it jumps—soccer, the paint on the wall, charts I was supposed to look at—and I’m trying to pull it back but it doesn’t stay. That’s kind of what brings me in, I guess. I can’t keep it in a straight line anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people would probably say it sounds unlikely. But I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like moved me around to see how I’d react, and they watch me on the unit cameras to check if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, because nights are always short and then someone calls out, and then the schedule gets all messy, and I know that happens, but it doesn’t feel random. It feels like they’re looking at me specifically. Like the camera is there anyway, but it’s… it’s for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisors are watching you through the cameras for any reason other than to ensure patient safety or manage the unit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know the cameras are supposed to be for safety, that’s what everyone says. But it doesn’t feel like just that. It feels like they’re watching me specifically, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I’ll mess up when they move my shifts around.\n\nAnd people keep saying it’s staffing, like someone called out or nights are short, and I get that, I’ve worked those nights, you know, the whole floor gets weird when you’re down one person… but I’m sure they changed mine on purpose. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. I know people say, “Oh, we’re short, somebody called out,” and that happens, I get it, I’ve been there when the whole unit is running around and everyone’s annoyed because nobody brought enough coffee or whatever—but it feels like mine gets changed for a reason.\n\nLike they’re watching how I handle it. The cameras too… they’re supposed to be for the unit, for safety, but I feel like they’re on me. Like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. I’ll be trying to answer one thing, like something from the overnight shift, and then I’m suddenly on soccer, or the wall color, or charts, and I can see their faces change because they don’t know how I got there. Then I try to fix it and it kind of makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you notice your thoughts or words becoming disorganized like that?\n\nPatient: A lot. I mean, when I notice it happening, I get this tight feeling because I can see people getting lost, like their eyes change, and then I’m trying to pull the sentence back but it’s already gone somewhere else. I’ll start with the overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer or paint colors or patient charts, and I know that’s not what I meant to say. It makes me really anxious because I can’t keep it in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, sometimes I think I hear something and then it’s just the hallway, or the carts, or somebody talking near the nurses’ station, and then I start thinking about whether I checked the room or not. But seeing things, like, I don’t think so. I’m not sure what counts, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts organized when you're not talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not talking, it’s like that in my head too. I’ll try to think about one thing, like what happened on the overnight shift, and then it jumps—soccer, the paint on the wall, charts I was supposed to look at—and I’m trying to pull it back but it doesn’t stay. That’s kind of what brings me in, I guess. I can’t keep it in a straight line anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people would probably say it sounds unlikely. But I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like moved me around to see how I’d react, and they watch me on the unit cameras to check if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, because nights are always short and then someone calls out, and then the schedule gets all messy, and I know that happens, but it doesn’t feel random. It feels like they’re looking at me specifically. Like the camera is there anyway, but it’s… it’s for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisors are watching you through the cameras for any reason other than to ensure patient safety or manage the unit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know the cameras are supposed to be for safety, that’s what everyone says. But it doesn’t feel like just that. It feels like they’re watching me specifically, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I’ll mess up when they move my shifts around.\n\nAnd people keep saying it’s staffing, like someone called out or nights are short, and I get that, I’ve worked those nights, you know, the whole floor gets weird when you’re down one person… but I’m sure they changed mine on purpose. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. I know people say, “Oh, we’re short, somebody called out,” and that happens, I get it, I’ve been there when the whole unit is running around and everyone’s annoyed because nobody brought enough coffee or whatever—but it feels like mine gets changed for a reason.\n\nLike they’re watching how I handle it. The cameras too… they’re supposed to be for the unit, for safety, but I feel like they’re on me. Like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when there's no one around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like someone standing there talking to me, I don’t think. Sometimes I hear something and I turn my head, but then it’s maybe a cart in the hallway or the vents, because those vents make weird sounds, especially at night.\n\nAt home it’s quieter, so I notice every little noise. That’s kind of what brings me in, I guess. I can’t keep it in a straight line anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that might seem strange or unlikely to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess people would probably say it sounds unlikely. But I’m pretty sure my supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like moved me around to see how I’d react, and they watch me on the unit cameras to check if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, because nights are always short and then someone calls out, and then the schedule gets all messy, and I know that happens, but it doesn’t feel random. It feels like they’re looking at me specifically. Like the camera is there anyway, but it’s… it’s for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisors are watching you through the cameras for any reason other than to ensure patient safety or manage the unit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know the cameras are supposed to be for safety, that’s what everyone says. But it doesn’t feel like just that. It feels like they’re watching me specifically, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I’ll mess up when they move my shifts around.\n\nAnd people keep saying it’s staffing, like someone called out or nights are short, and I get that, I’ve worked those nights, you know, the whole floor gets weird when you’re down one person… but I’m sure they changed mine on purpose. It’s like they’re testing me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen around you, like changes in your schedule or things you see on TV, are specifically related to you or are about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… with the schedule stuff, definitely. Like when they switch me from one shift to another or put me on nights again, it doesn’t feel like just a schedule. I know people say, “Oh, we’re short, somebody called out,” and that happens, I get it, I’ve been there when the whole unit is running around and everyone’s annoyed because nobody brought enough coffee or whatever—but it feels like mine gets changed for a reason.\n\nLike they’re watching how I handle it. The cameras too… they’re supposed to be for the unit, for safety, but I feel like they’re on me. Like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.\n\nIt’s more at the unit. Like, I’m sure the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the cameras. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but that feels too easy, like that’s just what they say so I don’t notice. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when there's no one around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like someone standing there talking to me, I don’t think. Sometimes I hear something and I turn my head, but then it’s maybe a cart in the hallway or the vents, because those vents make weird sounds, especially at night.\n\nAt home it’s quieter, so I notice every little noise. The fridge clicks and then I’m thinking about the unit again, and whether I forgot something, and then I lose track of what I was doing. But voices… I’m not sure I’d call it voices. I haven’t really noticed that like a clear person talking when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to complete tasks that don't involve talking, like following a recipe or assembling something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like if it’s something quiet where I’m supposed to just read and do it, I get stuck.\n\nI can read the same page of a patient note, or even a bill at home, like three times, and I still don’t really know what it said. My eyes go over the words, but it doesn’t… land, I guess. Then I start over, and then I get annoyed because it feels like I’m wasting time.\n\nEven at the store, just picking what to buy can freeze me up. Like I’ll stand there looking at two things, and it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just groceries, but my head won’t sort it out. Then people are trying to get around me, and I feel stupid, so sometimes I just leave with nothing or grab the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard something that you knew was not real, like a voice talking to you when no one was around?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t think so. Not like I knew for sure, “that’s not real.” Sometimes I hear a noise and my brain fills it in, like it almost sounds like somebody said something, but then I’ll listen and it’s just the pipes or somebody’s TV through the wall, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard because at work everything echoes, and you get used to listening for people calling out, so even when I’m home I kind of… stay tuned in, if that makes sense. But a voice talking to me with nobody there, I can’t say that’s happened. At least not clearly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. It’s stupid because I know the routine. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. So yeah, some things happening around me feel like they’re about me specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that seem unusual or out of character for you, like acting strangely in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like yelling in the street or anything, but at work I’ve noticed stuff. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.\n\nIt’s more at the unit. Like, I’m sure the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the cameras. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but that feels too easy, like that’s just what they say so I don’t notice. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when there's no one around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like someone standing there talking to me, I don’t think. Sometimes I hear something and I turn my head, but then it’s maybe a cart in the hallway or the vents, because those vents make weird sounds, especially at night.\n\nAt home it’s quieter, so I notice every little noise. The fridge clicks and then I’m thinking about the unit again, and whether I forgot something, and then I lose track of what I was doing. But voices… I’m not sure I’d call it voices. I haven’t really noticed that like a clear person talking when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to complete tasks that don't involve talking, like following a recipe or assembling something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like if it’s something quiet where I’m supposed to just read and do it, I get stuck.\n\nI can read the same page of a patient note, or even a bill at home, like three times, and I still don’t really know what it said. My eyes go over the words, but it doesn’t… land, I guess. Then I start over, and then I get annoyed because it feels like I’m wasting time.\n\nEven at the store, just picking what to buy can freeze me up. Like I’ll stand there looking at two things, and it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just groceries, but my head won’t sort it out. Then people are trying to get around me, and I feel stupid, so sometimes I just leave with nothing or grab the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard something that you knew was not real, like a voice talking to you when no one was around?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t think so. Not like I knew for sure, “that’s not real.” Sometimes I hear a noise and my brain fills it in, like it almost sounds like somebody said something, but then I’ll listen and it’s just the pipes or somebody’s TV through the wall, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard because at work everything echoes, and you get used to listening for people calling out, so even when I’m home I kind of… stay tuned in, if that makes sense. But a voice talking to me with nobody there, I can’t say that’s happened. At least not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, without any disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess, but not much lately. That’s kind of why I came in… I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see people’s faces change because they’re not following me. Like I’ll be in the hallway and realize I’m rocking back and forth, or I start copying a patient’s tapping without meaning to.\n\nAnd then sometimes I’m supposed to be doing rounds, but I’m just standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I forgot the next step. It’s embarrassing, because I know what I’m supposed to be doing. I’ve done it a hundred times. The hall lights are really bright too, and people are always moving around, and then I’m just… stuck there for a second.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing everyday tasks or chores because of your thoughts or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. It’s not just like forgetting to take out the trash, though that’s part of it. At work I’ve had to ask people to double-check my rounds and my charting because I’ll think I did it right and then I’m not sure, or I get stuck rechecking it. And then I feel stupid because I’m supposed to be helping, not making more work for everybody.\n\nAt home it’s… worse, honestly. Bills pile up, I don’t answer friends, and my apartment got really bad. Like dishes and clothes and mail everywhere. I keep telling myself I’ll do it after one more shift, but then the shift changes and I’m exhausted and I just sit there.\n\nIt got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I told them I’d been thinking about suicide. I didn’t really mean to say it like that, it just came out, but… yeah, things haven’t been getting done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by rocking back and forth or copying a patient's tapping without meaning to?\n\nPatient: It’s like… I don’t always notice I’m doing it at first. I’ll be in the hallway at work, supposed to be doing rounds, and then I realize I’m sort of rocking on my feet, back and forth. Not huge, just enough that if someone saw me, they’d probably think I was being weird.\n\nAnd the tapping thing… if a patient is tapping on the chair or the bedrail, sometimes I’ll start doing it too, like my hand just picks it up. I don’t mean to copy them. It just happens, and then I notice and stop because I feel embarrassed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be standing there with gloves halfway on, just stuck, like I know I’m supposed to keep moving and do rounds, but for a second my body doesn’t go to the next step. It’s stupid because I know the routine. I’ve done it so many times.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you consider unusual or that others have pointed out as strange, beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I mean… beyond that, not really, or I don’t know what counts as “strange.” It’s mostly the work stuff I notice because there are people around to see it.\n\nLike I’ll catch myself rocking in the hallway, or my hand starts doing the same tapping a patient is doing, and then I realize it and stop. Or I’ll be standing there with my gloves halfway on, like I froze or got stuck, even though I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds. It’s weird because I know the routine, but my body just doesn’t move for a second.\n\nI keep thinking maybe everyone does little things like that when they’re tired, but then I feel like people are looking at me, and then I start thinking about how I must look, and it just makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, or is it always somewhat disorganized?\n\nPatient: Not always, no. Sometimes I can talk normal, like if it’s simple, like “do you need water” or “I’m going to get linens,” that kind of thing. But when I try to explain something bigger, it’s like I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nI’ll start telling somebody about an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see their face change like they’re trying to follow me and they can’t. Then I try to back up, but backing up makes it worse because I remember another thing and jump there too. It’s embarrassing. I know what I mean in my head, or I think I do, but it comes out all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a conspiracy against you or that you are being targeted in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “conspiracy” is the word, because that sounds dramatic, but it feels like something is happening.\n\nThe supervisors keep changing my shifts, and people keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re putting me in certain spots to watch how I act, like to see if I’m unsafe or if I mess up.\n\nAnd the unit cameras… I know they’re there for patients and safety and all that, but I feel like they’re watching me through them too. Like if I stand too long in one place or take the wrong hallway, someone is checking. I try not to look up at them because then I start thinking about it more, and then I look weird, and then that probably makes them watch more.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or referring to you?\n\nPatient: TV or newspapers… I don’t really watch the news much, honestly. It gets loud and then I start thinking about work anyway.\n\nIt’s more at the unit. Like, I’m sure the supervisors are changing my shifts on purpose and watching me through the cameras. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but that feels too easy, like that’s just what they say so I don’t notice. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then if I see something on a screen in the break room, like some story about hospitals or safety, I kind of… I don’t know, my mind goes right back to that. But mostly it’s the shifts and the cameras. That’s the part that feels like it’s about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when there's no one around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like someone standing there talking to me, I don’t think. Sometimes I hear something and I turn my head, but then it’s maybe a cart in the hallway or the vents, because those vents make weird sounds, especially at night.\n\nAt home it’s quieter, so I notice every little noise. The fridge clicks and then I’m thinking about the unit again, and whether I forgot something, and then I lose track of what I was doing. But voices… I’m not sure I’d call it voices. I haven’t really noticed that like a clear person talking when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to complete tasks that don't involve talking, like following a recipe or assembling something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like if it’s something quiet where I’m supposed to just read and do it, I get stuck.\n\nI can read the same page of a patient note, or even a bill at home, like three times, and I still don’t really know what it said. My eyes go over the words, but it doesn’t… land, I guess. Then I start over, and then I get annoyed because it feels like I’m wasting time.\n\nEven at the store, just picking what to buy can freeze me up. Like I’ll stand there looking at two things, and it shouldn’t be hard, it’s just groceries, but my head won’t sort it out. Then people are trying to get around me, and I feel stupid, so sometimes I just leave with nothing or grab the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard something that you knew was not real, like a voice talking to you when no one was around?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t think so. Not like I knew for sure, “that’s not real.” Sometimes I hear a noise and my brain fills it in, like it almost sounds like somebody said something, but then I’ll listen and it’s just the pipes or somebody’s TV through the wall, maybe.\n\nIt’s hard because at work everything echoes, and you get used to listening for people calling out, so even when I’m home I kind of… stay tuned in, if that makes sense. But a voice talking to me with nobody there, I can’t say that’s happened. At least not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when your thoughts and words are completely organized and normal, without any disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess, but not much lately. That’s kind of why I came in… I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like an overnight shift, and then I’m talking about soccer, or the paint color in the hallway, or patient charts, and I can see people’s faces change because they’re not following me. And then I try to fix it, but fixing it makes it worse, because I add more stuff. So… organized and normal? Maybe for a little bit, but it doesn’t stay that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever clearly heard a voice or seen something that you knew was not really there?\n\nPatient: Not clearly, no… I mean, not where I could say, “That’s a voice,” or “I’m seeing something that isn’t there.” Sometimes I get unsure for a second, like I’ll look twice at a shadow or think I heard someone, but then it’s just… regular stuff, like a door, or someone in the hallway, or my eyes being tired.\n\nSo I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly had that happen.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
